`jobs.schedules_enabled: false` (test profile) registers no cron schedules; tests enqueue jobs directly. Installation-wide jobs (`scheduler.tick`, `packs.lifecycle`, `audit.verify-chain`, `cleanup.expired`) run under `withPlatform` with platform scope (F.10); every other job runs inside `withTenant`.

### E.3 Freshness and integrity derivation

One module, `apps/server/src/modules/read-model/derive/freshness.ts` (WP-17), used by the read model, the commands and the jobs.

```text
period(frequency)            = pack evidence_frequency_rule.period_days (0 for per event)
thresholds(ms, frequency)    = freshness_threshold row (system, then organisation), else grace 1, aging 1.25, expired 2
band(produced_at, p)         = 'fresh'    if p = 0 or age <= p + grace
                               'aging'    if age <= p * aging + grace
                               'expired'  if age <= p * expired
                               'critical' otherwise              (age = today - produced_at in days)
next_due(e)                  = e.next_due_override, else produced_at + p when p > 0, else null
integ(e)                     = C.12 mapping of its storage object (ok when no storage object)
valid(e)                     = C.12 "valid for status logic", using link_status_since and link_status_reason
                               for the 7 day, 72 hour (credential_invalid) and 72 hour (access_denied) windows
br01_link_ok(e)              = no storage object, or link_status in (intact, moved)        (BR-01 v2.0, TS 6.1)
integrity_status(e)          = A-05 mapping (ok, degraded, broken)

entryEvidenceState(entry)    = over the non-deleted evidence targeting the entry:
  candidates   = evidence with band in (fresh, aging) and valid(e)
  best         = candidate with the latest produced_at, else the evidence with the latest produced_at
  satisfiesBr01 = exists candidate with br01_link_ok(e) and produced_at within the current operating period
                  (today - produced_at <= period + grace, or any date for per-event evidence)
  keepsImplemented = exists candidate                      (TS 6.1 automatic downgrade when false)
  stored: last_evidence_at = best.produced_at, freshness_status = band(best), integrity_status = integrity_status(best)
```

`freshness.recompute` stores `freshness_status`, `integrity_status` and `derived_at` for each affected evidence and, for each applicability entry targeted by it, the `entryEvidenceState` columns. An entry in `implemented` whose `keepsImplemented` is false moves to `partially_implemented` (actor system, audit action `freshness.downgrade`, notification `freshness_downgrade` to the owner with BR-11 or BR-31 text) and gets a BR-11 task (expired or critical evidence) or a BR-31 task (unverifiable file) on the evidence, owned by the evidence owner. Tasks close when the evidence is valid again. Promotion back is never automatic. The verify command checks `satisfiesBr01`, so the same function decides both directions, and an entry verified on an older valid item is not downgraded because a newer item breaks.

### E.4 Link health check

Selection (TS 5.5): priority 1 is storage objects behind evidence of `implemented` entries, due when `last_verified_at` is older than 24 hours; priority 2 is evidence in queued or building evidence packs, 24 hours; priority 3 is every other evidence and document version object, 7 days. Repositories in `read_only` are still checked (TS 5.7).

Per object, the handler calls gateway `stat` with a per-repository token bucket (m365 4 requests per second, google_drive 5, managed_vault 50) and sets the new status with this precedence (A-06):

```text
if outcome = rate_limited:                 status unchanged; reschedule after Retry-After (TS 5.7: throttling never affects users)
elif outcome = unreachable:                status = 'verification_pending' unless the current status is already non-valid;
                                           link_status_reason = detail = credential_invalid ? 'credential_invalid' : 'unreachable'
elif outcome = not_found:                  status = 'missing'
elif outcome = forbidden:                  status = 'access_denied'
elif hash_source = 'provider' and current.version = pinned and current.hash <> stored hash:
                                           status = 'hash_mismatch'
elif current.version <> pinned:            status = pinnedVersionAvailable ? 'version_changed' : 'pinned_version_lost'
elif current.folderPath <> stored folder:  status = 'moved'
else                                       status = 'intact'
always: when found, store the current folder path and set last_good_check_at
```

On a status change: `link_status_since = now()`, a `link_health_result` row, audit action `link_status.changed`, BR-32 notification to the evidence owner for `version_changed` on evidence (anomaly, no downgrade), a "new document version available" notification to the document owner for document objects (PRD 11.4). `rate_limited` reschedules the rest of the batch after `Retry-After`. A failed token request (expired or revoked credential) is reported by the gateway as `unreachable` with `detail: credential_invalid`; objects become `verification_pending` with `link_status_reason = 'credential_invalid'`, which counts as valid for 72 hours only (TS 5.7, C.12), and `link-health.repository` sets the repository to `revoked`. The UI still shows `pending`, as in the sample.

### E.5 Reminders (synchronous part)

`POST /reminders` (WP-35) checks BR-50 (`reminder` rows for the same `recipient_user_id` and `record_key` in the last 72 hours), inserts `reminder` and a `notification` (kind `reminder`, text "Reminder: <action> for <title>, due <date>"), and enqueues `notifications.dispatch` in the same transaction. `POST /reminders/bulk` does the same per distinct non-unassigned holder of the open chain items of the system and returns `count` sent and the throttled list.

### E.6 Notifications

| Kind | Created by | Recipient | Digest |
|---|---|---|---|
| `reminder` | reminders | holder | no |
| `freshness_downgrade` | `freshness.recompute` | entry owner | no |
| `evidence_aging` | `freshness.sweep` | evidence owner | yes |
| `evidence_anomaly` (BR-32) | `link-health.check` | evidence owner | yes |
| `document_new_version` | `link-health.check` | document owner | yes |
| `document_review_due` | `documents.review-alerts` | document owner | 60 and 30 days yes, 14 and 7 days no |
| `document_expired` (BR-10) | `tasks.overdue` | document owner | no |
| `task_overdue` | `tasks.overdue` | prep task or cycle item owner | no |
| `finding_overdue` (BR-15) | `tasks.overdue` | finding owner and MS Managers | no |
| `prep_task_assigned` | `audit-prep.generate`, commands | task owner | yes |
| `audit_date_changed` (BR-42) | `PATCH audit-events` | holders with items now after the audit | no |
| `drift_detected` (BR-48) | `drift.evaluate` | drift row holder | yes |
| `connector_revoked` (TS 5.7) | `link-health.repository` | Tenant Admins | no |
| `credential_expiring` | `link-health.repository` | Tenant and Storage Admins | no |
| `licence_state`, `licence_expiring` | `license.check` | Tenant Admins | no |
| `edition_withdrawn` (BR-33) | `edition.withdrawn-alerts` | MS Managers of affected systems | no |
| `move_request` (BR-30) | move requests | Tenant Admins | no |
| `role_grant_pending` | role grants waiting for approval | other Tenant Admins | no |
| `export_ready`, `evidence_pack_ready` | export and pack jobs | requester | no |
| `account` | invitations, password reset, guest one-time code, MFA reset | the user (email only, no in-app row for anonymous flows) | no |
| `security` | factor enrolled or replaced, recovery code used, second factor locked, password changed or reset, identity linked | the user | no |

`notifications.dispatch` sends pending non-digest notifications through Nodemailer (`mail.smtp`), in the recipient's language preference (default English), subject `[MSCD] <title>`, plain text and simple HTML bodies, with a link to the app (`http.public_url`). Failures increment `email_attempts`; after 5 attempts `email_status = 'failed'`. In-app delivery is the `notification` row itself (`GET /notifications`). Notifications are only created for members of the organisation (`MembershipService`). `account` and `security` emails are sent synchronously by `AccountMailer` (WP-11) through the same transport, because they can concern users without a tenant context.

### E.7 Evidence pack build

1. Scope: evidence of the system that targets entries in scope (`scope.clauses` or `scope.codes`, default all), produced within the current certification cycle, plus evidence soft-deleted after the cycle start (flagged, A-09).
2. Pack folder `/Compliance/_packs/<MS code>/<event short label or YYYY-MM-DD>/` (TS 5.4), created with gateway `ensure-folder` in every repository that holds pack evidence.
3. Evidence whose status is `intact` or `moved` is copied with gateway `copy-within` into the pack folder of its own repository; everything else goes to the exceptions list with its reason (BR-34, A-10).
4. Manifest CSV (columns `evidence_code, requirement_codes, produced_at, external_version_id, hash_algorithm, hash_value, link_status, copy_location, deleted, exception_reason`) and manifest PDF (pdf-lib: cover with system, event and date; summary counts; evidence table; exceptions section) are written to the pack folder of the system's default repository with gateway `PUT /v1/objects` and recorded as storage objects.
5. For the Managed Vault, a `.tar.gz` archive of the copied files and both manifests is stored as one more object (PRD 11.5).
6. `evidence_pack` and `evidence_pack_item` rows (each copied item records `copy_storage_object_id`, a `storage_object` row for the copy in the pack folder, so external auditors open copies only, D-34), `background_task` result, notification `evidence_pack_ready`, audit `evidence_pack.built`. Manifests and the archive are downloaded through `GET /ms/{ms}/evidence-packs/{id}/download` (F.13).

### E.8 Pack installation and file formats

Pack file `<pack_id>-<version>.pack` is a gzipped tar of:

| File | Content |
|---|---|
| `manifest.json` | `pack_id`, `pack_version`, `framework_code`, `framework_name`, `edition`, `status` (`current`, `transition`, `withdrawn`), `pre_release`, `supersedes?: {edition, transition_ends}`, `engine_min_version`, `modules[]`, `applicability_target`, `catalogue` (boolean), `datasets: {"<file>": "sha256:<hex>"}` |
| `requirements.csv` | `requirement_code, parent_code, title_short, clause_group, is_hs_common, documented_info_mandatory, applicability_assessable, default_frequency_code, evidence_example, sort_order` |
| `catalogue.csv` | `item_code, theme_code, title_short, requirement_code, default_frequency_code, evidence_example, sort_order` |
| `chain.csv` | `stage_key, position, label_en, label_id, question_en, question_id, source, entity_type, risk_profile_code, is_mandatory, clause_at_risk, overview_clause, gate_type_label_en, gate_type_label_id` |
| `activities.csv` | `phase_no, phase_name_en, phase_name_id, is_gate_phase, activity_no, title, clause_ref, output_document_code, expected_outcome` |
| `frequencies.csv` | `code, label_en, label_id, period_days, sort_order` |
| `labels.en.json`, `labels.id.json` | flat key to text: `ms.name`, `ms.long_name`, `ms.manager_role`, `gate.name`, `applicability.artefact_name`, `risk.object_name`, `catalogue.theme.<n>` |
| `cycle_requirements.csv` | `code, requirement_code, title, derivation, sort_order` |
| `drift_rules.json` | array of `{code, label_en, label_id, severity, source_stage, source_kind, predicate, message, sort_order}` (`source_kind` one of `manual`, `stale_risk`, `dd_expired`, `metric`, `exercise`, `unverif`, `nohash`) |
| `prep_tasks.csv` | `code, title, days_before_audit, skip_on_first_audit, sort_order` |
| `review_inputs.csv`, `finding_conditions.csv`, `document_templates.csv`, `audit_activities.csv` | as the `ref` columns of C.5 (may hold only the header row; `audit_activities.csv`: `code, event_type, clause_ref, title, auditee_role, key_questions, evidence_to_request, sort_order`) |
| `lookups.csv` | `domain, code, label_en, label_id, sort_order` (`ref.lookup_value`; every pack carries at least the domains `doc_type`, `treatment_option` and, where used, `exercise_type`, `bribery_risk_level`, `due_diligence_subject`) |
| `rules.json` | array of `{rule_id, active, params}` |
| `risk_profiles.json` | array of `{code, definition}` |
| `mappings/*.csv` | `source_code, target_pack_id, target_code, mapping_type, evidence_reusable, note` |
| `signature` | `ed25519:<base64 signature over the exact bytes of manifest.json>`, first line; second line `key:<key id>` |

Key id is the first 16 hexadecimal characters of the SHA-256 of the public key in DER (SPKI) form. Licences are JSON documents with the TS 2.5 fields plus `key_id`; the signature is Ed25519 over the canonical JSON (keys sorted recursively, no whitespace, `signature` removed) and stored as `"signature": "ed25519:<base64>"`.

Install steps (`PackInstaller`, WP-12):

1. Read the archive in memory before trusting anything: gunzip and untar with a decompressed size cap of 200 MB and at most 500 entries; only regular files; no absolute paths, no `..` segments, no duplicate names (compared after normalisation); every file at the archive root or under `mappings/`. Any violation rejects the pack with no row written.
2. Verify the `signature` file against the pack keys compiled into the build (D-16; a release build also rejects development key ids). Verify that the archive holds exactly the files listed in `manifest.datasets` plus `manifest.json` and `signature`, and every dataset checksum.
3. Validate the manifest and every row with the zod schemas of `packages/shared/src/pack`; check `engine_min_version` against the server version; check references (catalogue to requirement codes, chain entity types to known tables, lookup domains used by templates, activities' output codes may be absent).
4. Reject a `pack_version` lower than the installed one for the same `pack_id` (semver compare); the same version is a no-op.
5. In one transaction as `mscd_pack_installer`: upsert `ref.framework`, insert or update `ref.framework_version` (a newer `pack_version` of the same edition updates requirement and catalogue titles, labels, lookups and templates in place and adds rows; it never deletes rows; tenant activity copies are untouched, D-30), insert child rows, set a superseded edition to `transition` with `transition_ends_at`, write `platform.pack_install` (`installed`) and `platform.audit_log` (`pack.installed`, with `organization_id` of the requesting Tenant Admin in single mode, null in multi mode). Any error rolls back and records `failed` with the message in a separate transaction, plus `pack.install_failed` in the audit log.

With `tenancy.mode = multi`, `POST /admin/packs/import` and `packs install` are platform operator actions; Tenant Admins see installed editions (`pack.read`) and choose them for their systems (`pack.activate`).

## F. Security

### F.1 Sessions

| Item | Rule |
|---|---|
| Cookie | Name `__Host-mscd_session` (`Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, no `Domain`). With `security.cookie_secure: false` (demo and test over plain HTTP only) the name is `mscd_session` without `Secure` |
| Value | 32 random bytes, base64url. The database stores only `sha256(value)` in `platform.session.id_hash` |
| States | `mfa_pending` (first factor accepted, second factor missing), `mfa_enrolment` (no confirmed factor yet), `active`. Only `active` passes the session guard for business routes |
| Organisation pin | `auth_org_id` is the organisation whose policy accepted the sign-in: the provider's organisation for OIDC and SAML, the grant's organisation for guests, and for local sign-in the user's default organisation among those whose `local_login` allows the user. `organization_id` (the current organisation) may change only to organisations that accept the same authentication (D-26, F.7) |
| Timeouts | Idle 60 minutes (`identity.session.idle_minutes`), absolute 8 hours; with "Keep me signed in" (`remember: true`) the cookie gets `Max-Age` and the absolute limit is 12 hours (`identity.session.remember_hours`), matching the login design. `last_seen_at` and `idle_expires_at` are refreshed at most once per minute. Guest sessions: idle 30 minutes, absolute 4 hours |
| Rotation | A new session id (old row revoked) after password success, after MFA success, after enrolment, after `PUT /me/org`, after persona switch and after any change of the user's own roles |
| Revocation | Logout; password change (every other session of the user); password reset (every session); user disabled (every session); MFA reset (every session); MFA re-enrolment (every other session); removal from an organisation or membership disabled (sessions whose `organization_id` or `auth_org_id` is that organisation); auditor grant revoked or expired (sessions with that `auth_grant_id`); identity provider disabled (sessions with that `identity_provider_id`) |
| Storage | PostgreSQL in both job drivers (D-19) |

### F.2 CSRF

- Synchronizer token: 32 random bytes per session, hash stored in `csrf_token_hash`, value returned in `Me.csrfToken` and in `LoginResponse`. Every non-GET request with a session must send `X-CSRF-Token`; compared in constant time. Failure: 403 `csrf`.
- Public POST routes (login, password reset, invitation accept, guest start and login, test endpoints) check that `Origin` (or `Referer` when `Origin` is missing) matches `http.public_url`. The SAML ACS is exempt from the header and protected by the SAML binding cookie, `InResponseTo`, the assertion id cache and the signature (F.9). The OIDC and repository OAuth callbacks are GET routes protected by the hashed `state`, the browser binding cookie and the transaction's user (F.8); they change nothing unless all three match.
- `SameSite=Lax` cookies and no CORS (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin` never sent) add depth.

### F.3 Rate limits

`@fastify/rate-limit` with a PostgreSQL store (`platform.rate_limit_bucket`, fixed window, `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET hits = hits + 1`), or Valkey when `jobs.driver = bullmq`. Keys use `request.ip`, which is trustworthy only with the F.12 proxy rules.

| Key | Limit |
|---|---|
| `POST /auth/login` per IP | 30 per 15 minutes |
| `POST /auth/login` per normalised email | 10 per 15 minutes; after 10 consecutive password failures the account is locked for 15 minutes (`locked_until`); responses stay generic |
| `POST /auth/mfa/verify` per session | 5 per 5 minutes; the session is revoked after 10 failures |
| Second factor per user, across all sessions | `app_user.mfa_failed_count` counts consecutive TOTP and recovery code failures; at 10 the second factor locks (`mfa_locked_until`) for 15 minutes, doubling with every further lock (`mfa_lock_level`, at most 24 hours); while locked every second-factor attempt returns 429 without checking the code; a success resets the count (not the level, which decays by one per 24 hours without a lock); every failure audits `auth.mfa_failed`, every lock audits `auth.mfa_locked` and emails the user (`security`) |
| Password reset request per email and per IP | 5 per hour |
| Guest code start or login per IP | 10 per hour; per grant 5 per hour; 5 code attempts per transaction |
| Calendar feed per token | 60 per hour |
| Authenticated API per user | 600 per minute |
| Authenticated API per organisation | 6,000 per minute |
| Exports, evidence packs, link health runs per user | 10 per hour |
| Uploads per user | 60 per hour, maximum 100 MiB per file (`storage.max_upload_bytes`) |

### F.4 Passwords and local sign-in

- argon2id through `@node-rs/argon2`: memory 19,456 KiB, 2 iterations, parallelism 1 (OWASP minimum), output 32 bytes; rehash on login when parameters change.
- Policy: 12 to 256 characters, not equal to the email or handle, not in the 10,000 most common passwords (`apps/server/src/modules/identity/common-passwords.txt`, shipped in the image).
- Local sign-in policy per organisation (`organization.local_login`, set by the Tenant Admin with `PATCH /admin/organization`): `allowed`, `admins_only` (only members with a Tenant or Storage Admin role) or `disabled`. A local sign-in succeeds only when at least one active membership allows it for the user; the session is pinned to that organisation (F.1). With a correct password and no such membership the answer is 401 with `messageKey: 'auth.sso_required'` and the providers to use. A password reset request for such a user sends no email (the response is still 202). So a user disabled in the customer's IdP cannot keep working through a local password where the organisation requires SSO.
- Reset: token of 32 random bytes, hash stored, valid 30 minutes, single use, email link `<public_url>/reset-password#token=...` (fragment, never logged); success revokes every session of the user and audits `auth.password_reset`; a change audits `auth.password_changed`; both email the user (`security`).
- Invitations (D-25): `platform.invitation`, 72 hours, single use, link `<public_url>/invite#token=...`. Redeemed either by setting a password (`POST /auth/invitations/accept`, then MFA enrolment) or by signing in through one of the inviting organisation's providers with `?invitation=<token>` (F.8 linking path a).

### F.5 Multi-factor authentication

- Every user needs a second factor (PRD 13): local accounts and guests through TOTP; SSO users through the IdP's MFA proof or, when it is missing, the local TOTP step.
- TOTP per RFC 6238: SHA-1, 6 digits, 30 second step, accepted window of one step before and after. Secret 20 random bytes, shown once as base32 and `otpauth://totp/MSCD:<email>?secret=...&issuer=MSCD`, stored sealed with `platform.key` (`mfa_factor.secret_sealed`, AAD `mfa:<userId>`).
- Replay protection: `last_used_step` stores the last accepted step; a code for that step or an earlier one is rejected.
- Recovery codes: 10 codes of 10 characters (`xxxx-xxxx-xx`, lowercase base32), argon2id-hashed, single use, shown once at enrolment. Using one audits `auth.recovery_code_used` and emails the user (`security`).
- Enforcement: a user without a confirmed factor cannot reach `active`: the first sign-in goes to `mfa_enrolment`. A Tenant Admin may reset the factor of a user whose only active membership is in that organisation; otherwise only the platform operator may (F.6). A reset revokes the user's sessions and emails the user.
- Re-enrolment from an active session requires the current TOTP code or the password in the request, unless the session verified one of them in the last 5 minutes (`session.password_at`, `session.mfa_at`). Confirming a new factor replaces the old one, emails the user, audits `auth.mfa_enrolled` with `after_data.replaced: true`, and revokes the user's other sessions.
- SSO users (OIDC): with `require_mfa_claim` (default `true`) the `amr` claim proves MFA only when it contains `mfa`, or at least two distinct values that are both in `accepted_amr`. SAML: the assertion's `AuthnContextClassRef` must be in `saml_mfa_authn_contexts`. When the proof is missing: `mfa_fallback = 'local_totp'` (default) continues to the local TOTP step (`mfa_pending`, or `mfa_enrolment` when the user has no factor); `reject` fails with `mfa_required_by_policy` (ARCHITECTURE 10.1). `require_mfa_claim = false` is allowed only together with `mfa_fallback = 'local_totp'`, so every SSO sign-in still ends with a second factor.
- Guests: emailed one-time code first (F.14), then TOTP enrolment on first use and TOTP on every later sign-in.

### F.6 Permission matrix

Business roles per system: MM `ms_manager`, PO `programme_owner`, DO `domain_owner`, RO `risk_owner`, IA `internal_auditor`, TM `top_management`, EA `external_auditor`, CI `compliance_investigator`. Organisation roles: TA `tenant_admin`, SA `storage_admin`. Platform role: OP `platform_operator`. "own" means only records whose owner or holder is the user (for `risk.residual.accept`: the risk's `owner_user_id`; for create routes: the new record's owner). The matrix lives in `packages/shared/src/authz/matrix.ts`; the guard, `Me.permissions` and the generated RBAC tests read the same constant. A row that lists an organisation role for an ms-scoped permission grants it in every system of the organisation (F.7).

| Permission | Scope | MM | PO | DO | RO | IA | TM | EA | CI | TA | SA | OP |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ms.read` | ms | yes | yes | own rows | own rows | yes | yes | projection (D-34), time-boxed, every request logged | own rows | | | |
| `applicability.complete` | ms | yes | | own | own | | | | | | | |
| `applicability.verify` | ms | yes | yes | | | | | | | | | |
| `applicability.manage` | ms | yes | | | | | | | | | | |
| `activity.transition` | ms | yes | | own | own | | | | | | | |
| `activity.verify` | ms | yes | yes | | | | | | | | | |
| `phase.verify` | ms | yes | | | | | | | | | | |
| `record.assign` | ms | yes | yes | | | | | | | | | |
| `record.complete_action` | ms | yes | | own | own | | | | own | | | |
| `record.delete` | ms | yes | | | | | | | | | | |
| `document.review` | ms | yes | | own | | | | | | | | |
| `document.approve` | ms | yes | yes | | | | | | | | | |
| `document.manage` | ms | yes | | | | | | | | | | |
| `document.version.create` | ms | yes | | own | | | | | | | | |
| `evidence.file.submit` | ms | yes | | own | own | | | | | | | |
| `evidence.manage` | ms | yes | | own | own | | | | | | | |
| `register.manage` (context, interested parties, assets, metrics) | ms | yes | | own | own | | | | | | | |
| `metric.record` | ms | yes | | own | own | | | | | | | |

| `ops.read`, `ops.jobs.run`, `ops.users.manage` | platform | | | | | | | | | | | yes |

Administrative roles carry no business permission (TS 7.1). Risk Owner and Compliance Investigator follow A-16 (Risk Owner adds residual acceptance on own risks); Programme Owner rights are the A.4 decision.

Tenant Admin limits (TS 7.2):

- `user.manage` changes only the organisation's `org_membership` row (status, admin roles). Global account attributes (display name, job title, account status, password, MFA factor, email) change only by the user, by the platform operator (`ops.users.manage`), or by a Tenant Admin when the user has no other active membership.
- `POST /admin/users` answers the same 202 for a new and an existing email; for an existing account it creates an `invited` membership that the user accepts after signing in (`POST /me/memberships/{org}/accept`), so other tenants' accounts are never revealed.
- A role grant to the actor, or to a user who holds `tenant_admin` or `storage_admin` in the organisation, is created with `approval_status = 'pending_approval'` and needs approval by a different Tenant Admin who is not the grantee (C.13). With a single Tenant Admin such a grant cannot be activated, and the response says so (as A-50). Every grant to an admin user is audited with `after_data.grantee_admin_roles`.

### F.7 Authorisation guard

- `AuthzGuard` is a global Nest guard (WP-39; WP-08 provides the token and a deny-all default). It reads the route's `RouteDef` metadata: `auth` level, `permission`, `scope`.
- Session resolution (WP-11, `SESSION_RESOLVER`): cookie, then `platform.session` read with platform scope (state and expiry), then the current organisation (`session.organization_id`, membership `active`, organisation `active`, or `offboarding` for `GET` and exports only, else 403 `organisation-inactive`), then `platform.app_user` (status active). `TenantResolver` caches organisations for 60 seconds and drops the entry on every status change made through the CLI or operator routes.
- System resolution for `scope: 'ms'`: the `:ms` path parameter when the route has one; only routes without it read `ms` from the body (`POST /reminders`, `POST /reminders/bulk`, move requests) or the query (browse, storage objects). Unknown slug gives 404.
- Role resolution: `user_ms_role` rows for (user, system) that are not deleted, `approval_status = 'active'` and not past `valid_until`; organisation roles from `org_membership.admin_roles`; platform roles from `platform_role`. An organisation role listed in an ms-scoped row grants that permission in every system of the organisation (for example `link_health.run` for SA). "MM of any system" rows need the business role in at least one system. Cached per request.
- Decision: allowed when any role grants the permission. When the only granting role is an "own" grant, the guard sets `request.authz.ownOnly = true` and the service checks ownership through `RecordResolver` before acting; a mismatch returns 403.
- `RecordResolver.resolve(ms, entityType, id, {forUpdate})` (WP-39): entity type from `RECORD_ENTITY_TYPES` (D.1), row loaded inside the tenant transaction, then `management_system_id = :ms` or, for shareable types, an `entity_ms_link` row to `:ms`; anything else is 404. Every command of WP-20, WP-21, WP-35, WP-36 and WP-37 uses it; none queries a record by id directly.
- `MembershipService.assertMember(userId)` (WP-39): active member of the organisation (guests included only where the column allows them) or 422 `NOT-A-MEMBER`; person handles in requests are resolved only among members. The `assert_member` trigger (C.4) is the backstop.
- `ScopePolicy` (WP-39), applied by every read path of D-04:
  - Owner scoping: when the user's roles in the system are only own-scoped (DO, RO, CI), lists and snapshots keep only records whose owner or holder is the user, plus the records directly referenced by them (up and down codes) as `{code, title}` stubs; counts are computed on the full data first (D-04).
  - Classification: documents and evidence classified `restricted` are visible only to their owner, MS Managers and Programme Owners of a linked system; `confidential` is hidden from external auditors unless the item is in one of their evidence packs.
  - External auditors (D-34): the projection of G.5 (`EaProjection`); storage objects only through `evidence_pack_item.copy_storage_object_id` of `ready` packs of the audited system; no `web_url`, no holder names on pending items, no pending-per-person data.
  - Top Management: no storage object access; pending data as aggregates only.
  - `canReadObject(user, ms, storageObjectId)`: the object must be referenced by a record of `ms` that the user can see under the rules above (evidence, document version, pack manifest or copy); otherwise 404.
  - `/ui/org`: `TITLES`, `CHAINS` and `CLAUSE_AT_RISK` only for systems where the user holds a role (and, with only own-scoped roles there, only for visible records); `PEOPLE` only members holding a role in one of those systems.
- External auditors: allowed only on `GET` routes of the audited system while `valid_until` is in the future and the grant is not revoked; every request writes `auditor.read` to the tenant audit trail with the route id and the record refs returned (BR-20, TS 7.1).
- Denials: every 403 on a business route writes `authz.denied` (route id, permission, system) to the tenant audit trail, at most one entry per user, route and minute (BR-28 requires rejected access to be logged; the cap keeps a scripted client from flooding the trail; the denial counter metric counts all).
- Licence: read-only mode (BR-37) is enforced in the same guard for non-GET routes not marked `readOnlyAllowed`; features through `LicenceGate.assertFeature` in the services (D-15).
- UI role per system (`Me.uiRoleByMs`): `manager` for MM or PO, `auditor` for IA or EA, `top` for TM, `owner` for DO, RO or CI; the first match in that order wins.

### F.8 OIDC sign-in, identity linking and OAuth consent

1. `GET /auth/oidc/{slug}/start`: load the enabled provider (it always belongs to one organisation); discovery through `openid-client` (`discovery(issuer, clientId, clientSecret)`, cached one hour; with `identity.oidc.allow_insecure_http: true`, a demo and test profile key, `allowInsecureRequests` is applied so plain-HTTP issuers such as the test Keycloak work); create `auth_transaction` (`kind` `oidc_login`, or `oidc_link` for `POST /me/identities/{slug}/link` with `user_id` set) with hashed `state`, `nonce`, sealed PKCE verifier (S256), `return_to` (relative path only), the hashed invitation token when `?invitation=` is given (`target_id` = invitation id), and a browser binding: a random value in cookie `mscd_auth_tx` (`HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, 10 minutes) whose hash is stored. Redirect to the authorisation URL (`response_type=code`, scopes from the provider).
2. `GET /auth/oidc/{slug}/callback`: `DELETE ... RETURNING` the transaction by state hash (single use), check the binding cookie and expiry, run `authorizationCodeGrant` with the expected state, nonce and verifier (the library validates issuer, audience, expiry and signature).
3. Subject: `sub`, or for `subject_mode = 'entra'` the pair `<tid>:<oid>`, where `tid` must equal `entra_tenant_id` (else `idp_error`). Email is used only when the token has `email` and `email_verified === true` (absent counts as unverified; Entra ID tokens therefore never qualify).
4. Identity (D-25), first match wins:
   - `user_identity (provider, subject)` exists: that user.
   - `oidc_link` transaction: link the subject to the transaction's user (`link_method = 'session'`), audit `identity.linked`, email the user (`security`).
   - Invitation transaction: the invitation must be unused, unexpired, of the provider's organisation; when the token has a verified email it must equal the invitation email (case-insensitive); link (`link_method = 'invitation'`), mark the invitation used and the membership active.
   - JIT: provider has `jit_provisioning`, every domain of `allowed_email_domains` is in `platform.verified_domain` for the provider's organisation, the verified email's domain is one of them, and no non-guest account has that email. A new user and an active membership without roles are created after `LicenceGate.assertCanAddUser` (`link_method = 'jit'`).
   - Otherwise `no_account` (also when an account with that email exists: it must be linked through an invitation or from a signed-in session, never by email).
5. MFA (F.5), then session with `auth_method = 'oidc'`, `identity_provider_id`, `amr`, `auth_org_id` = the provider's organisation (D-26); audit `auth.login` with provider and subject; redirect to `return_to`.
6. Errors redirect to `/login?error=<code>` (`no_account`, `mfa_required_by_policy`, `idp_error`, `expired`, `invitation_invalid`); the login page maps codes to messages.

Keycloak stands in for Entra ID in tests (realm `mscd`, confidential client `mscd`, I.3). Real Entra ID needs a provider row with issuer `https://login.microsoftonline.com/<tenant>/v2.0`, `subject_mode = 'entra'` and `entra_tenant_id`.

Repository OAuth consent (WP-13, `oauth_refresh_token` credentials): `POST /admin/storage/repositories/{id}/reauthorize` creates an `auth_transaction` of kind `repo_oauth` (hashed state, PKCE verifier, `organization_id`, `user_id` = the Tenant or Storage Admin, `target_id` = repository id, browser binding cookie, 10 minutes) and returns the consent URL. `GET /admin/storage/repositories/oauth/callback` deletes the transaction by state hash and requires the same signed-in user and binding cookie, then calls gateway `credentials/oauth-exchange` (D.5), which checks that the new account can reach the configured drive and root before sealing; the api stores the sealed blob and audits `storage.credential.set` with `after_data.externalAccount`.

### F.9 SAML sign-in scope

In this build: SP-initiated login only, AuthnRequest through HTTP-Redirect, response through HTTP-POST to the ACS, signed assertions required (`wantAssertionsSigned`), audience equals the SP entity id, `InResponseTo` checked against `auth_transaction.saml_request_id`, 60 second clock skew. Subject: the persistent NameID (`urn:oasis:names:tc:SAML:2.0:nameid-format:persistent`); assertions with any other NameID format fail with `idp_error`. Identity mapping, invitations, linking and JIT exactly as F.8 step 4 (SAML assertions never prove `email_verified`, so SAML JIT is not offered). MFA from `AuthnContextClassRef` (F.5).

- Browser binding: `/start` sets a separate cookie `mscd_saml_tx` (`HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=None`, `Path=/api/v1/auth/saml/`, 10 minutes), because browsers do not send `SameSite=Lax` cookies on the cross-site POST from the IdP. The ACS requires it and compares its hash with the transaction; a post without it fails. With `security.cookie_secure: false` (plain-HTTP demo and test) the cookie has no `Secure` and `SameSite=Lax`, so SAML in a real browser needs HTTPS; Vitest suites send the cookie themselves.
- The transaction is deleted atomically at the ACS (`DELETE ... RETURNING`), and the assertion `ID` is stored in `platform.saml_assertion_seen` until `NotOnOrAfter` plus the skew; a second use of the same assertion fails.
- SP metadata at `GET /auth/saml/{slug}/metadata`. Not in this build: encrypted assertions, IdP-initiated login, single logout, artifact binding, signing of AuthnRequests. Tested against a Keycloak SAML client in the same realm.

### F.10 Tenant scoping per request

```ts
// apps/server/src/modules/tenancy/tenant-scope.ts (WP-08)
async withTenant<T>(ctx: { orgId: string; schema: string; userId: string; requestId: string },
                    fn: (trx: Transaction<DB>) => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
  if (!/^org_[a-z0-9_]{1,59}$/.test(ctx.schema)) throw new Error('bad schema');
  return this.db.transaction().execute(async (trx) => {
    await sql`select set_config('search_path', ${`"${ctx.schema}", ext`}, true),
                     set_config('app.org_id', ${ctx.orgId}, true),
                     set_config('app.user_id', ${ctx.userId}, true),
                     set_config('app.request_id', ${ctx.requestId}, true)`.execute(trx);
    return fn(trx);
  });
}
// withPlatform(fn, { scope: 'auth' | 'operator' | 'job' | 'cli' }) sets app.platform_scope = 'on' (transaction local)
// for the platform tables of D-31; without it those tables show only the rows of app.org_id and app.user_id.
```

- Every tenant read and write goes through `withTenant`; lint rules (WP-03, `eslint.config.mjs`): `no-restricted-syntax` on calls of `selectFrom`, `insertInto`, `updateTable`, `deleteFrom`, `executeQuery` and `.execute(...)` whose receiver or argument is the injected pool (`db` or `this.db`, never a transaction `trx`) outside `src/db`, `src/modules/tenancy` and `src/seed`; calls of `withPlatform` with platform scope only in `src/modules/{identity,ops,jobs,audit,packs,license,tenancy}`, `src/cli`, `src/seed` and `src/worker`; any SQL string literal containing `SET ` without `LOCAL` (and any `set_config(..., false)`) is an error.
- The settings are transaction-local. The pool has a checkout guard (WP-08, `src/db/pool-guard.ts`): when a connection is acquired outside `withTenant`, `current_setting('app.org_id', true)` and `current_setting('app.platform_scope', true)` must be empty and `search_path` must be the role default; otherwise the connection is destroyed and an error is logged. In test profiles the guard runs on every checkout; in production on every hundredth checkout and on every error.
- RLS is the second layer (D-02, D-31); the tenant policy also binds each schema to its own organisation id (C.4), so a wrong `app.org_id` or a schema-qualified statement into another tenant's schema reads and writes nothing. Tests in J.5 prove it.

### F.11 Keys, secrets and the audit chain

**Key provider and SecretBox**

```ts
export interface KeyProvider {           // apps/server/src/modules/keys (api, worker) and src/gateway/crypto (gateway)
  readonly kekId: string;                // first 8 hex of sha256(KEK)
  wrap(plain: Buffer, aad: string): Promise<string>;
  unwrap(wrapped: string, aad: string): Promise<Buffer>;
}
// one driver in this build: 'file' (D-09); other kms.provider values are rejected by the profile schema
```

- `file` driver: the KEK is 32 random bytes, base64, in a file (`kms.file.path`, mode 0400). `kms.file.previous_paths` lists older KEKs for unwrapping during rotation.
- SecretBox format: `v1.<kekId>.<base64url iv(12 bytes)>.<base64url ciphertext and 16 byte tag>`, AES-256-GCM, additional authenticated data names the purpose and binding: `mfa:<userId>`, `idp:<providerId>`, `dek:<orgId>`, `cred:<orgId>:<repositoryId>:<configSha256>` (D-08), `obj:<orgId>:<objectId>`, `pkce:<transactionId>`.
- `mscd-server keys generate --out <dir>` writes `platform.key`, `storage.key`, `gateway.token`, `audit-anchor.key` and `audit-anchor.pub` (Ed25519). `mscd-server keys rewrap` re-wraps tenant data keys and re-seals secrets under the current KEK. `mscd-server keys rotate-gateway-token` (D.5).
- Vault object encryption (D-10): content key per object, sealed with the tenant data key (`obj:<orgId>:<objectId>`); chunks of 64 KiB, nonce = 4-byte random prefix plus 8-byte chunk counter, AAD = object id plus chunk index plus a final-chunk flag (truncation is detected); header stored as S3 metadata `x-amz-meta-mscd-enc` (`{"v":1,"alg":"A256GCM-CHUNK64K","ck":"<sealed>","np":"<prefix>"}`). SHA-256 is computed over the plaintext while encrypting.

**Audit trail**

- Written by `AuditWriter.record(trx, entry)` (WP-08) in the same transaction as the change. Entries hold actor, action, entity type, id and business code, the system, changed fields only in `before_data` and `after_data`, the active edition (`<pack_id> <version>`, two editions during a transition), request id, source IP and user agent.
- Redaction before writing: values of keys matching `password`, `secret`, `clientSecret`, `privateKeyPem`, `certificatePem`, `refreshToken`, `token`, `code`, `otp`, `sealedBlob`, `*_sealed`, `*_hash` and `wrappedDek` are replaced by `"[redacted]"` in `before_data` and `after_data` (`src/common/audit-redaction.ts`, unit-tested with every request schema of D.2 that carries a secret).
- Actions: `auth.login`, `auth.login_failed`, `auth.logout`, `auth.mfa_enrolled`, `auth.mfa_failed`, `auth.mfa_locked`, `auth.mfa_reset`, `auth.recovery_code_used`, `auth.password_reset`, `auth.password_changed`, `auth.session_revoked`, `auth.persona_switch`, `auth.org_switched`, `auth.guest_code_sent`, `auth.guest_login`, `identity.linked`, `identity.registered`, `idp.created`, `idp.updated`, `idp.deleted`, `user.invited`, `user.created`, `user.updated`, `membership.updated`, `org.settings_updated`, `role.granted`, `role.grant_requested`, `role.grant_approved`, `role.grant_rejected`, `role.revoked`, `tenant.created`, `tenant.domain_verified`, `licence.installed`, `licence.rejected`, `licence.state_changed`, `licence.clock_rollback`, `pack.installed`, `pack.install_failed`, `pack.rejected`, `ms.created`, `ms.updated`, `ms.transition`, `applicability.created`, `applicability.updated`, `applicability.transition`, `activity.transition`, `phase.verified`, `record.assigned`, `record.action_completed`, `record.deleted`, `register.created`, `register.updated`, `risk.treatment_transition`, `risk.residual_accepted`, `metric.value_recorded`, `evidence.created`, `evidence.updated`, `evidence.target_added`, `evidence.target_removed`, `entity_link.created`, `entity_link.removed`, `finding.created`, `finding.updated`, `finding.transition`, `corrective_action.created`, `corrective_action.transition`, `self_assessment.updated`, `audit_activity.updated`, `management_review.created`, `management_review.input_updated`, `management_review.transition`, `document.created`, `document.updated`, `document.transition`, `document.version_created`, `evidence.file_linked`, `evidence.file_uploaded`, `file.opened`, `file.downloaded`, `reminder.sent`, `task.completed`, `audit_event.created`, `audit_event.updated`, `prep_task.updated`, `cycle_item.updated`, `storage.repository.created`, `storage.repository.updated`, `storage.credential.set`, `storage.credential.revoked`, `storage.routing.requested`, `storage.routing.approved`, `storage.routing.rejected`, `storage.move_requested`, `link_status.changed`, `freshness.downgrade`, `evidence_pack.built`, `evidence_pack.downloaded`, `export.created`, `export.downloaded`, `calendar_feed.created`, `calendar_feed.revoked`, `auditor_access.granted`, `auditor_access.revoked`, `auditor.read`, `authz.denied`, `ops.job_run`, `ops.diagnostics_exported`. Platform-level actions (`auth.*`, `identity.*`, `idp.*`, `user.*`, `membership.*`, `tenant.*`, `licence.*`, `pack.*`, `ops.*`) go to `platform.audit_log` with the organisation id when there is one; the rest to the tenant `audit_trail`.
- Reading: MS Managers and time-boxed external auditors read full entries of their system (`audit_trail.read`); Tenant Admins read metadata only (`audit_trail.metadata.read`, `AuditEntryMeta`), because before and after values are business data (TS 7.1, PRD 10.2, PRD 13). The `audit_trail` export type requires `audit_trail.read`.
- Chain verification (both tables, run by `audit.verify-chain` and `GET /admin/audit-trail/verify`):

```sql
WITH c AS (
  SELECT seq, prev_hash, row_hash,
         lag(row_hash) OVER (ORDER BY seq) AS expected_prev,
         lag(seq) OVER (ORDER BY seq) AS prev_seq,
         sha256(prev_hash || convert_to(platform.audit_canonical_text(seq, id, occurred_at, organization_id,
           management_system_id, actor_user_id, actor_kind, action, entity_type, entity_id, entity_code,
           before_data, after_data, edition, request_id, source_ip, user_agent), 'UTF8')) AS expected_hash
  FROM audit_trail)
SELECT count(*) AS checked,
       min(seq) FILTER (WHERE row_hash <> expected_hash
                           OR prev_hash <> coalesce(expected_prev, '\x'::bytea)
                           OR seq <> coalesce(prev_seq, 0) + 1) AS first_broken_seq
FROM c;
```

- Anchors (D-33): the job then compares the newest anchor of each chain with the stored row of that `seq` (detects removal of the newest rows) and writes a new anchor: payload `{chain, seq, rowHash, anchoredAt, installationId}` as canonical JSON, signed with the Ed25519 key in `audit-anchor.key` (mounted only into the worker; key id as in E.8). The signed anchor is (1) logged as one JSON line `{event: 'audit.anchor', ...}` for the customer's log platform or SIEM, (2) written to the vault bucket under `audit-anchors/<chain>/<date>.json` through gateway `/v1/anchors` (object lock when the store supports it), and (3) inserted into `platform.audit_anchor`; `GET /admin/audit-trail/anchors` returns the signed lines for offline checks with `audit-anchor.pub`.
- What this proves, stated in `docs/INSTALL-ONPREM.md` (WP-33): the hash chain detects any change made through the application role; together with anchors kept outside the database (log platform, object storage with object lock, downloaded anchor files) it also detects a rewrite by the database owner or schema owner, who cannot forge a signed anchor for a rewritten chain.

### F.12 HTTP hardening

- `@fastify/helmet` on the api: `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; sandbox` on every `/api` response (JSON never needs more; file responses tighten it further, F.13), `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`.
- The web image keeps its CSP (same origin only); WP-18 adds `connect-src 'self'` and `img-src 'self' data:` for the MFA QR code, and keeps the optional fonts switch.
- Body limits: 1 MiB JSON, 50 MiB pack import, upload streams limited by the intent's `max_bytes`.
- Client address: `http.trust_proxy` is `{hops: 0}` (no proxy) or `{cidrs: [...]}` (never `true`, never a hop count above 0: Fastify 5.12 removed hop-count trust after GHSA-3m5p-2c4r-xxw2), passed to Fastify's `trustProxy`. Compose: the edge is the web NGINX, which sets `X-Forwarded-For $remote_addr` (overwriting anything the client sent) and runs on a fixed address of the Compose network (`172.31.250.10` on subnet `${MSCD_SUBNET:-172.31.250.0/24}`), so the api trusts `{cidrs: ['172.31.250.10/32']}`. Helm: `/api` is routed through the web NGINX as well; NGINX trusts the ingress controller with `set_real_ip_from <ingress.trustedCidrs>`, `real_ip_header X-Forwarded-For` and `real_ip_recursive on`, then sends `X-Forwarded-For $remote_addr`; the api trusts `{cidrs: [<web pod CIDR>]}` (Helm value `api.trustedProxyCidrs`) and a NetworkPolicy admits api traffic only from web pods. A request that reaches the api from any other address keeps its socket address as `request.ip` whatever headers it sends (J.5 test).
- Request ids: `X-Request-Id` accepted only when it matches `^[A-Za-z0-9-]{8,64}$`; NGINX sets `X-Request-Id $request_id`.
- Logging: pino redaction of `req.headers.cookie`, `req.headers.authorization`, `req.headers["x-csrf-token"]`, `req.headers["x-mscd-put"]`, `*.password`, `*.secret`, `*.clientSecret`, `*.refreshToken`, `*.sealedBlob`, `*.privateKeyPem`, `*.certificatePem`, `*.token`, `*.code`, `*_sealed`; the pino-http request serializer rewrites `req.url`, replacing the values of the query parameters `token`, `code`, `state`, `t`, `SAMLResponse`, `RelayState` and `invitation` with `[redacted]`. The NGINX `log_format mscd` (WP-18) logs `$uri` plus a redacted copy of `$args` built with `map` rules for the same parameter names. The operator diagnostics archive takes log lines only from the already-redacted ring buffer.
- No stack traces in problem responses; `internal` problems carry `requestId` only.

### F.13 File responses

`FileResponder.send(reply, {stream, storedType, fileName, size, inline})` (WP-08, `src/common/file-response.ts`) is the only way any route returns file bytes (storage object content, export downloads, evidence pack manifests and archives, the calendar feed is `text/calendar` and also passes through it):

- `Content-Type`: the stored type only when it is in the inline-safe allow-list `application/pdf`, `image/png`, `image/jpeg`, `image/gif`, `image/webp`, `text/plain` (sent as `text/plain; charset=utf-8`), `text/csv`, `text/calendar`, or one of the Office types; everything else, including `text/html`, `image/svg+xml` and `application/xml`, is sent as `application/octet-stream`. The stored type is the gateway's detection (D.5), never the client's declaration.
- `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<ASCII fallback>"; filename*=UTF-8''<percent-encoded>`, with control characters, quotes, slashes and backslashes removed and the name cut to 150 characters. `inline` is used only when the request asks for it and the type is PDF or one of the four raster image types.
- `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cache-Control: no-store`.
- Each response writes `file.downloaded` (or `file.opened` for `open` and `redirect`) to the tenant audit trail.
- A separate file origin is not used in this build; the headers above make same-origin serving safe for the allowed types (Appendix 1 lists a dedicated download origin as hardening for later).

### F.14 Guest auditor access

1. An MS Manager creates a grant (`POST /ms/{ms}/auditor-access`, feature `auditor_guest_access`): a guest account (`kind = 'guest'`, handle `guest-<8 hex>`, the grant email, `mfa_required = true`, no password), an `active` membership, a `user_ms_role` `external_auditor` with `valid_until` = grant expiry, and a 32-byte token whose SHA-256 is stored. The link `<public_url>/guest#<token>` is emailed to the auditor (`account`) and never returned to the requester.
2. The SPA's `/guest` page reads the fragment, removes it from the address bar and posts it to `POST /auth/guest/start`. A valid, unused, unexpired, unrevoked grant marks `link_used_at` (the link works once), creates an `auth_transaction` of kind `guest_otp` bound to a new cookie `mscd_guest_tx` (10 minutes), and emails a 6-digit code to the grant address.
3. `POST /auth/guest/verify` with the code (5 attempts) creates a session pinned to the grant (`auth_method = 'guest'`, `auth_grant_id`, `auth_org_id`) in `mfa_enrolment` on first use, else `mfa_pending`; the guest then enrols or enters TOTP (F.5).
4. Later visits: `/guest` offers "Send me a code" (`POST /auth/guest/login {email}`), which sends a new code when an active grant exists for that email; then TOTP as in step 3.
5. Revocation (or expiry) of the grant revokes every session with that `auth_grant_id`, sets `valid_until = now()` on the role row, and disables the guest membership; audited `auditor_access.revoked`.
6. An auditor who already has a regular account is never linked to the grant. If the organisation wants to use that account instead, a Tenant Admin adds a membership and an `external_auditor` role with `valid_until` explicitly (F.6), and the auditor signs in with their own credentials and MFA.

### F.15 Spreadsheet exports

CSV and XLSX writers (exports, register exports, audit trail CSV) neutralise formulas: a cell whose text starts with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return is prefixed with a single quote in CSV, and written as a string cell (`{ t: 's' }`, never a formula) in exceljs with the same prefix. Numbers and dates are written as typed cells. A unit test round-trips `=HYPERLINK("http://x")` and `@SUM(1)` through both formats.

## G. Demo seed

### G.1 Flow

```text
apps/web/src/data (sample module + sample-derive.js + SampleDataSource, WP-07)
        │  node apps/web/scripts/export-sample-snapshot.mjs            (WP-14)
        ▼
apps/server/src/seed/demo/sample-snapshot.json     tools/mock-providers/fixtures/demo.json
        │                                                    │
        │  mscd-server seed demo                             │  mock-providers --fixtures demo
        ▼                                                    ▼
PostgreSQL: platform users, organisations, licences,   mock Graph and Drive state that matches
packs (from packs/dist), tenant rows                   the seeded storage objects and picker files
```

- `mscd-server seed demo [--reset]` runs as `mscd_migrator` (plus `mscd_pack_installer` for packs) and refuses to run unless `demo.enabled` is true. `--reset` drops the demo organisations' schemas and rows first. The command is idempotent: a second run without `--reset` exits with "already seeded".
- `npm run seed:demo` (root) runs bootstrap, migrate and seed against `MSCD_DATABASE_URL` with the demo profile.
- The seed reads packs from `packs.dir` (`packs/dist` in development). `packs/dist`, `packages/pack-tools/dist` and `packages/shared/dist` are git-ignored, so every script that needs them (`npm run seed:demo`, `apps/server/test/setup/global-db.ts`, `apps/web/e2e-api/stack.mjs`, `tools/smoke`) first runs `node tools/scripts/ensure-built.mjs` (WP-14), which builds `@mscd/shared` and `@mscd/pack-tools` and runs `build-all` with the development pack key when the outputs are missing or older than their sources.
- `node apps/web/scripts/export-sample-snapshot.mjs --check` regenerates both JSON files in memory and fails when they differ from the committed files (CI guard against drift between the sample and the seed).

### G.2 Snapshot export

The export script imports the pure sample modules (`src/data/mscd-data.js`, `src/data/sample-orgs.js`, `src/data/sample-derive.js`, `src/data/SampleDataSource.js`; WP-07 guarantees they import no React and no CSS) and writes:

```jsonc
{
  "today": "2026-09-29",
  "orgs": [ /* the three sample organisations */ ],
  "snapshots": { "ndt": { /* SampleDataSource.load('ndt') with ref objects replaced by keys */ }, "bsd": {}, "rmn": {} },
  "raw": { "PHASES": {}, "PHASE_STATE": {}, "OUTPUT_MAP": [], "PICKER_FILES": {}, "FREQ": {}, "TERA_KEYS": [] },
  "literals": { "gateName": {}, "gtSuggestions": {}, "docApprover": {} }   // the hard-coded switches of UI 2.14
}
```

`literals` are read from the optional MS fields that WP-07 adds in sample mode (`ms.gateName`, `ms.gtSuggestions`, `ms.approver`), so the controller and the export share one source.

### G.3 Organisations and users

| Organisation | Slug, schema | Systems | `demo_deploy_label` | Sites |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PT Nusantara Digital Teknologi | `ndt`, `org_ndt` | isms, qms, bcms, abms | `saas` | 2 |
| PT Bank Sentosa Digital | `bsd`, `org_bsd` | isms, qms | `onprem` | 3 |
| RS Medika Nusantara | `rmn`, `org_rmn` | isms | `saas` | 1 |
| Test Organisation | `tst`, `org_tst` | isms | none | 1 |

Each sample organisation gets the full sample data of its systems (the sample reuses the same system records across organisations; the seed copies them into each schema). `tst` holds the ISMS data too and is used by API tests that mutate data, so the three sample organisations stay intact for E2E tests.

All demo and test users have the password `Mscd-Demo-2026!`. Their TOTP secret is `base32(HMAC-SHA256(key = "mscd-demo-totp", message = handle))[0:32]` (the first 20 bytes, RFC 4648 base32 without padding), so tests compute codes; `tools/scripts/demo-totp.mjs <handle>` prints the current code.

Organisations are created with fixed ids so the development licences can bind to them (D-15): `ndt` `00000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000a001`, `bsd` `...a002`, `rmn` `...a003`, `tst` `...a004` (`TenantProvisioner.create` accepts an optional `id`). All four use `local_login = 'allowed'`.

When the profile has `demo.keycloak` (`{issuer, client_id, client_secret_file}`; demo overlay and the SSO test profile), the seed creates the OIDC provider `keycloak` ("Sign in with Keycloak", icon `generic`) for `ndt` and `tst`, with the client secret sealed with `platform.key`, `require_mfa_claim: true` and `mfa_fallback: 'local_totp'` (Keycloak sends no MFA proof, so SSO users continue to the local TOTP step), and pre-registers the subjects of `rina`, `bagus`, `maya` and `hendra` (`user_identity`, `link_method = 'admin'`). Their Keycloak user ids are fixed in the realm file (WP-02): `rina` `00000000-0000-4000-8000-0000000000c1`, `bagus` `...c2`, `maya` `...c3`, `hendra` `...c4`. `sso.only` exists only in Keycloak (the `no_account` test).

Active non-guest members of each sample organisation: 11 sample people plus 75 staff = 86, which reproduces `LICENCE.users` "86 of 150". The demo personas (`ROLES`) are `manager: rina`, `owner: bagus`, `auditor: maya`, `top: hendra` with the sample labels.

The licence of each organisation (signed with the development licence key, bound to the organisation's fixed id): `license_id` `LIC-DEMO-<SLUG>`, `organization` = organisation name, `organization_id` = the fixed id, `valid_from` 2026-04-01, `valid_until` 2027-03-31, `grace_days` 30, `frameworks` [ISO27001, ISO9001, ISO22301, ISO37001], `max_management_systems` 10, `max_users` 150, `storage_adapters` [managed_vault, m365, google_drive, smb], `features` [integrated_mode, edition_transition, link_health_monitor, auditor_guest_access].

### G.4 Stored display values (D-06)

| UI value | Stored in | Seeded from |
|---|---|---|
| `MS.cert` | `management_system.display.certLabel` | sample `cert` |
| `MS.stage` | `display.stage` (pair) | sample `stage` |
| `MS.phase` | `display.phase` (pair) | sample `phase` |
| `MS.pack` | `display.packLabel` | sample `pack` |
| Golden Thread suggestions | `display.gtSuggestions` | `literals.gtSuggestions` |
| `MS.color` | `management_system.color` | sample `color` |
| `MS.clauses` | `clause_readiness` (7 rows) | sample `clauses` |
| `MS.banner` | `ms_alert` (kind `transition` for qms, `connector` for bcms, `beta` for abms) | sample `banner` |
| Requirement chain due date | `applicability_entry.action_due_at` | chain item `due` of kind `req` |
| Activity owner, due and verified dates, statuses | `implementation_activity_progress` | derived phases of the snapshot |
| Evidence produced date, version, hash algorithm, folder, integrity | `evidence`, `storage_object` | snapshot `evs` |
| Document approver | `document.approver_user_id` | `literals.docApprover` |
| Audit event names | `audit_event.name_en`, `name_id`, `short_label` | `MS.audit.name`, `EVENTS[].t` |
| Chain instances | `trace_chain_instance`, `trace_chain_node` | `CHAINS` |
| Record up and down lists | `record_reference` | `X[].up`, `X[].down` |
| Drift rows | `drift_result` | `DRIFT[].rows` |
| Repository texts | `storage_repository.display.configSummary`, `display.locName`, `connection_message_en`, `connection_message_id` | `REPOS[].tenant`, `LOC[][0]`, `REPOS[].msg` |
| Routing note | `storage_routing_rule.note` | `ROUTING[][3]` |
| Pack update text and install dates | `platform.pack_catalog`, `platform.pack_install.installed_at`, `ref.framework_version.installed_at` | `PACKS[]` |

### G.5 Read model rules and text formats (parity)

Formats: `fmtDay(d)` = `DD Mon YYYY` with a two-digit day and English month abbreviation (`02 Feb 2026`); `fmtDayTime(t)` = `DD Mon YYYY HH:mm` in the organisation time zone.

| Snapshot key | Rule |
|---|---|
| `orgs[]` | Memberships of the session user ordered by `position`: `{id: slug, name, ms: slugs of systems where the user holds a role, ordered by position, deploy: demo_deploy_label in demo mode else SystemProfile.deploy, sites: site_count}` |
| `MS[]` fields | `id` slug; `code` `ref.framework.name`; `ed` active edition, or `"<active> to <target>"` during a transition; `short` `management_system.code`; `name` labels `ms.long_name` (en, id); `mgr` handle of the primary `ms_manager`; `mgrRole` label `ms.manager_role` (en); `color`; `scope` `scope_statement`; `certifier` `certification_body`; `lead` next event `lead_auditor_name`, else `-`; `cert`, `stage`, `phase`, `pack` from `display`; `audit` next event `{name: [name_en, name_id], date: scheduled_at, days: audit_days, prepWindowDays: prep_window_days}` or null (TS 9.4: first event with `scheduled_at >= today`); `clauses` seven `readiness_pct` values for clauses 4 to 10; `findings` `{open: findings not closed with classification major, minor or ofi, major: findings not closed with classification major}`; `banner` newest open `ms_alert` as `{tone, text: [text_en, text_id], kind}`; `loc` default repository slug; `locMode` `Managed Vault` for a vault default, else `Customer Repository, ` plus the mode label of the system's default routing rule; `unit` `ctrl` when `applicability_target = catalogue_item`, else `req`; `theme` for `ctrl`: `{5: label catalogue.theme.5, ...}`; `gateName` label `gate.name`; `gtSuggestions` from `display` |
| `PEOPLE` | Members holding a business role in at least one system where the session user holds a role (F.7 `ScopePolicy`): `{handle: [display_name, job_title or '-']}`, plus `none: ['Unassigned', '-']` |
| `ROLES` | Demo mode: the four personas of G.3. Otherwise one entry per distinct UI role the session user holds in any system of the organisation, each `{who: <session handle>, label: <sample label of that UI role>}`, so `ROLES[uiRoleByMs[ms]].who` always resolves (H.5 step 5) |
| `LOC` | Per repository `slug: [display.locName or name, kind, short]` with kind `sp` (m365 organisational), `od` (m365 personal), `gd` (google_drive), `vault`, `smb`; short `SharePoint`, `OneDrive`, `Google Drive`, `Vault`, `File share` |
| `REPOS[]` | Repositories ordered by `position`: `id` slug, `ref` (uuid, used by the admin repository routes), `adapter`, `ownershipType`, `actions` (`test` always; `reauthorise` for `google_drive` with an `oauth_refresh_token` credential; `moveToLibrary` for `ownership_type = 'personal'`), `name`, `type` (`Microsoft 365`, `Google Workspace`, empty for vault, `File share`, `S3`, `WebDAV`), `modes` labels joined with `, ` (`stored` gives `Upload, versioned, SHA-256`; `link` `Link`; `pass_through` `Pass-through`; `zero_transit` `Zero-transit`), `status` per C.11, `msg` `[connection_message_en, connection_message_id]` when set, `tenant` `display.configSummary`, `ms` codes of systems that use the repository (default repository, routing target, or storage objects of their evidence) in system order, `last` `fmtDayTime(last_checked_at)` |
| `ROUTING[]` | Active default routing rule per system (classification and object kind null), system order: `[ms.code, repository name, mode label (stored gives Upload), note or '-']` |
| `PACKS[ms]` | Only with `pack.read` (otherwise the key is absent and the controller renders `-`, A.4). Versions the system uses (active and transition target), newest edition first: `v` = each `pack_version` plus ` (<edition>)` when there are two, plus ` (beta)` when `pre_release`, joined with `, `; `installed` `fmtDay` of the newest install; `eds` = edition alone when the framework has one installed edition, else every installed edition with ` current`, ` in transition` or ` withdrawn`, joined with `, `; `update` = `"<version>: <note_en>"` from `pack_catalog` for a newer version of the newest edition, else null; `status` `transition` (system has a target), `beta` (active version is pre-release) or `current` |
| `LICENCE` | `null` without `license.read` (A.4). Otherwise `org` licence `organization`; `to` `fmtDay(valid_until)`; `fw` `"<frameworks used> of <frameworks licensed>"`; `ms` `"<systems> of <max_management_systems>"`; `users` `"<active users> of <max_users>"`; `adapters` labels (`Managed Vault`, `Microsoft 365`, `Google Workspace`, `SMB`, `S3`, `WebDAV`) joined with `, `; `features` labels (`integrated_mode` Integrated Management System, `edition_transition` Edition Transition Assistant, `link_health_monitor` Link Health Monitor, `auditor_guest_access` Auditor guest access); `sig` `Ed25519, verified` |
| `STAGES[ms]` | Chain definitions by `position`: `{id: stage_key, l: [label_en, label_id], q: auditor_question_en, src}` with `src` `req` (applicability), `soa` (justification), `doc`, `ev`, absent for entity stages; `rec` for entity and justification stages = number of records (C.10) |
| `X[ms][stage]` | Open records of the stage in `position` order: `{id: code, t: title, act: pending_action, h: owner handle or 'none', due: action_due_at, gate: true when is_gate_item, up, down}` (`up` and `down` from `record_reference`) |
| `CHAINS`, `TITLES`, `CLAUSE_AT_RISK`, `EXAMPLES` | Limited by `ScopePolicy` to systems where the user holds a role (F.7). Chain instances as `{id: anchor_code, nodes: [[stage_key, record_code]]}`; titles of the visible chain records by code; `clause_at_risk` per stage key (first system in position order wins); `evidence_example` per requirement key |
| `EVENTS[ms]` | Events by `scheduled_at`: `{t: short_label, d: scheduled_at, days: audit_days, result: outcome_summary (when set), next: true for the next event}` |
| `FOCUS[ms]` | Next event `expected_focus`, else `-` |
| `PREP[ms]` | Prep tasks of the next event by `position`: `[title, owner or 'none', due_at, done or progress or open]` |
| `PAST[ms]` | Findings with `source = 'certification_audit'` by `position`: `[code, Major or Minor or OFI, clause_ref, title, closed or open, status_note]` |
| `CYCLE_ITEMS[ms]` | Items of the latest cycle by `position`: `[requirement_code, title, owner, due_at, status code, condition_note or '']` |
| `DRIFT[ms]` | Drift rule definitions by `sort_order`: `{id: code, l: [label_en, label_id]}` plus `rows` (open `drift_result` rows: `[entity_code, title, holder, due_at]`) for `rows`, `stale_risk` and `dd_expired` rules, or `src` for `unverif`, `nohash`, `metric`, `exercise` |
| `bundle.reqs` | Entries by `position`: `id` (`item_code` for catalogue items; `Cl. ` plus `requirement_code`), `key` (code without a leading `A.`), `title` `title_short`, `theme` (`A.<n> <theme label>` or `Clause <clause_group>`), `st` (C.11), `owner`, `doc` (first linked document code), `freq`, `note` `status_note`, `gate` `holds_gate`, `just` (inclusion or exclusion justification, or empty), `risks` (codes by position), `by` (handle of `completed_by_user_id` when set) |
| `bundle.docs` | Documents linked to the system, not org-wide first then org-wide, each by `position`: `id`, `title`, `tier` (label of `doc_type` from `ref.lookup_value`), `ver` (current version label or `-`), `st` (C.11), `appr` (`approved_at` or empty), `rev`, `owner`, `shared` (`is_org_wide` or linked to two or more systems), `ms`, `approver`, `path`, `hashAlg`, `usedBy` (codes of the other linked systems) |
| `bundle.evs` | Evidence linked to the system by `position`: UI 1.7 fields from E.3, `loc` repository slug, `ver` storage object `version_label`, `hashAlg` (C.12), `path` `folder_path`, `hash`, `lastGoodCheck` (`fmtDayTime(last_good_check_at)`), `lastCheck` (`fmtDayTime(last_verified_at)`, the UI 2.12 "Last check" column) |
| `bundle.chain`, `bundle.gates`, `bundle.phases` | UI 2.3, 2.4 and 2.7 over stored state, with D-12 and D-13; requirement items take `due` from `action_due_at` (null for blocked entries); `soa` items take holder and due from `justification_holder_user_id` (else owner) and `justification_due_at`. `GateItem.type` is a `Pair`: requirement gate items `['Control', 'Kontrol']` or `['Requirement', 'Persyaratan']` by `unit`, chain record gate items `[en, id]` pairs with the sample labels (`['Risk','Risiko']`, `['Treatment','Perlakuan']`, `['SoA','SoA']`, `['BIA','BIA']`, else the stage key twice); ChainItem.title, ChainItem.act and GateItem.why are `[en, id]` pairs too (orchestrator decision after WP-07, matching the WP-05 schema); the controller applies `li()` when it builds gate rows (H.5), so the language follows the UI |
| `refs` | `Record<RecordKey, RecordRef>` for every record the bundle shows, with these RecordKey kinds: `req` and `soa` (both `applicability_entry`; `soa` keys address the justification fields, D.2 `AssignmentRequest.kind`), `doc`, `ev`, `act` (`implementation_activity_progress`), `x` (the stage table of C.10), `prep` (`audit_prep_task`), `cycle` (`cycle_requirement_status`), `drift` (`drift_result`). Key format `<ms>:<kind>:<business id>` as UI 4.2 |
| External auditor projection (`EaProjection`, D-34) | `GET /ui/ms/{ms}` for a user whose only role in the system is `external_auditor` returns: `MS` fields, `bundle.reqs` without `owner`, `by`, `note`; `bundle.docs` without `owner`, `approver`, `path`; `bundle.evs` only for evidence in `ready` evidence packs of the system, without `owner`, `path`, `hash`; `bundle.chain` with `recs` only and no items; `gates`, `phases`, `X`, `PREP`, `CYCLE_ITEMS` and `DRIFT` empty; `EVENTS` and `PAST` as for other roles; `refs` only for the returned records. `GET /ui/org` returns only the audited system, `PEOPLE` without job titles, `REPOS`, `ROUTING`, `PACKS` and `LICENCE` empty |

### G.6 Row mapping

| Snapshot source | Rows written per organisation schema |
|---|---|
| `MS[i]` | `management_system` (slug, code = `short`, name = English name, framework version from the pack of G.7, scope, status `certified` when `cert` starts with `Certified`, else `implementing`, default repository = repository with slug `loc`, certification body, colour, `display`, position i); `management_system_version` rows (qms: 2015 `active`, 2026 `transition_target`); `certification_cycle` (from `cert`: certified date and cycle years; otherwise a `planned` cycle 2026 to 2029); `clause_readiness`; `ms_alert` from `banner`; `user_ms_role` per G.3 |
| `REPOS`, `LOC` | `storage_repository` per repository (sp: m365 organisational modes `[link, pass_through]`; od: m365 personal `[link]`; gd: google_drive `[zero_transit, link]`, `connection_status` `revoked` since `2026-09-27T06:00+07:00`; vault: managed_vault `[stored]`); `last_checked_at` parsed from `last`; `config` points at the mock drives (G.8) with `rootFolderId` = the `Compliance` folder and `roots` per system (`isms` `Compliance/ISMS`, `qms` `Compliance/QMS`, `bcms` `Compliance/BCMS`, `abms` `Compliance/ABMS`); credentials sealed through the gateway (development values, bound to the config, D-08) |
| `ROUTING` | One active default rule per system with `mode` from the label (`Upload` is `stored`) and `note` |
| `bundles[ms].reqs` | `applicability_entry` (target from the pack by code, status from C.11 reverse mapping, `holds_gate` for `blocked`, `is_applicable` false for `na`, justification texts, owner, note, `action_due_at` from the chain item of kind `req`, position); `applicability_entry_risk` (risk rows by code); `applicability_entry_document`; `operating_criteria` (`freq`) |
| `bundles[ms].docs` | `document` once per code (shared `IMS-01` to `IMS-04` once per organisation with `is_org_wide` and an `entity_ms_link` to every system, primary isms); `document_version` with a `storage_object` in the system's default repository (folder `Compliance / <short> / Documents`) when `ver` is not `-` (status `approved` for approved and expired documents, `in_review` for review, `draft` for draft); `entity_ms_link` |
| `bundles[ms].evs` | `evidence` (code, title, owner, `produced_at`, frequency, `file_sequence` from the version digits, `next_due_override` when `next` differs from `prod + p`); `storage_object` (repository slug `loc`, `external_item_id` `demo-<loc>-<code>`, `external_version_id` and `version_label` from `ver`, hash algorithm from `hashAlg` with a deterministic value `sha256("<code>")` in hex or base64 for quickXor, `folder_path` from `path`, `link_status` from C.12 reverse mapping); `evidence_target`; `entity_ms_link` |
| `X[ms][stage]` | Rows in the stage's table (C.10) with `pending_action`, owner, `action_due_at`, `is_gate_item`, position; `record_reference` for `up` and `down` |
| Explicit stage fillers | G.7 |
| `bundles[ms].phases` | `implementation_activity_progress` per activity, with the template fields copied from the pack (`title`, `clause_ref`, `output_document_code`, `expected_outcome`, `is_gate_phase`, `template_pack_version`, D-30): status reverse-mapped (`locked` stored as `not_started`), owner, `blocker_gate_item_ref` and `blocker_reason` ("Blocked by gate item <ref>"), `due_at` (non-verified date), `verified_at` and `completed_at` (verified date), `completed_by_user_id` null; `output_document_id` from the activity's output code when the document exists |
| `EVENTS`, `MS.audit`, `FOCUS` | `audit_event` per event (type from the label: Stage 1 `stage1`, Stage 2 `stage2`, Surveillance `surveillance`, Recertification `recertification`; names from `MS.audit.name` for the next event, else the short label in both languages; `outcome_summary` from `result`; `lead_auditor_name` from `MS.lead` for the next event; `expected_focus` from `FOCUS` for the next event) |
| `PREP` | `audit_prep_task` for the next event (status reverse-mapped, position) |
| `PAST` | `finding` with `source = 'certification_audit'`, linked to the latest past event, position; plus internal audit findings so the open and major counts equal `MS.findings` (isms: `IA-2026-01` major open and `IA-2026-02` minor open; qms: `IA-2026-03` minor open) |
| `CYCLE_ITEMS` | `cycle_requirement_status` of the current cycle |
| `DRIFT` | `drift_result` rows for rules with `rows` (holder, due, position) |
| `CHAINS` | `trace_chain_instance` and `trace_chain_node` |
| `PACKS` | `platform.pack_catalog` (qms `ISO9001-2026` 1.0.0, abms `ISO37001-2025` 0.7.0) and install dates |
| Tasks | None are written by the seed. `freshness.recompute` and `tasks.overdue` on the seeded data open BR-11, BR-31 and BR-10 tasks only for records that are already chain items of their owner, so `GET /tasks` counts stay as UI 4.7 (G.9) |

### G.7 Packs from the sample and filler records

Pack sources (`packs/sources/<pack_id>/`, WP-04) are extracted by `packs/scripts/extract-from-sample.mjs`:

| Pack | Content |
|---|---|
| `ISO27001-2022` 1.2.0 | Requirements: the Harmonized Structure list of the sample (clauses 4 to 10). Catalogue: the 93 Annex A titles with theme codes `A.5` to `A.8`, default frequency from the sample frequency function, evidence examples from `EXAMPLES`. Chain from `STAGES.isms` (soa stage `justification`, entity type `chain_record`). Activities from `PHASES.isms` (phase 4 gate) with output codes from `OUTPUT_MAP`. Labels: `ms.name` ISMS, `ms.long_name`, `ms.manager_role` ISMS Manager, `gate.name` Risk & SoA, theme labels |
| `ISO9001-2015` 1.4.2 and `ISO9001-2026` 0.9.0 | Requirements from the sample QMS list (Harmonized Structure plus clause 8 for qms); chain from `STAGES.qms` (`action` uses `risk_treatment`, `objective` uses `quality_objective`); activities from `PHASES.qms`; 2026 supersedes 2015 with `transition_ends` 2029-09-15 |
| `ISO22301-2019` 1.0.0 | Requirements from the BCMS list; chain from `STAGES.bcms` (`product` uses `prioritized_activity`, `bia` uses `business_impact_analysis`, `drisk` uses `risk` with profile `disruption`, `exercise` uses `exercise`) |
| `ISO37001-2025` 0.6.0 pre-release and `ISO37001-2016` 1.0.0 withdrawn | Requirements from the ABMS list; chain from `STAGES.abms` (`brisk` uses `risk` with profile `bribery`, `associate` uses `business_associate`) |

Every pack also carries `frequencies.csv` from `FREQ`, `cycle_requirements.csv` from the system's `CYCLE_ITEMS` titles (with an empty `derivation`, so `cycle.derive` keeps the seeded statuses), `drift_rules.json` from `DRIFT` (`risk12` gets `source_kind: stale_risk` with `predicate {"months": 12}`, `dd` gets `dd_expired`, `mr` and `csat` get `manual`, the `src` rules keep their kind), `lookups.csv` (`doc_type` from the sample document tiers, `treatment_option` `modify`, `retain`, `avoid`, `share`, `exercise_type` `tabletop`, `walkthrough`, `simulation`, `full` for ISO 22301, `bribery_risk_level` `low`, `medium`, `high`, `very_high` and `due_diligence_subject` `associate`, `position`, `transaction`, `project` for ISO 37001), `audit_activities.csv` and `finding_conditions.csv` (ISO 27001 only: six generic Stage 1 and Stage 2 activities and six generic conditions written by CyberLentera as short titles, listed in `packs/README.md`; the other packs carry the header row only, pack content depth is Appendix 1), `prep_tasks.csv` from `PREP` (`days_before_audit` = event date minus due date), and `rules.json` activating BR-01, BR-02 (27001 and 37001 only), BR-03, BR-04, BR-06 to BR-17, BR-21 to BR-23, BR-29 to BR-32, BR-34 to BR-50 (orchestrator decision after WP-04: the A.5 A-01 rules are activated in every pack so the WP-20 engine, which runs only activated rules, enforces them; BR-03 carries its phrase list per language in `params`). Overview clause per stage (UI 2.6): `context` and `brisk` 4; `risk`, `treatment`, `soa`, `action`, `objective` 6; `document` 7; `metric` 9; others 8. `clause_at_risk` from `CLAUSE_AT_RISK`.

Fillers: an entity stage must end up with exactly `rec` records. The seed first creates the open records of `X`, then ready records (no `pending_action`) for every code of that stage that appears in `CHAINS`, in requirement `risks`, in `DRIFT` rows or in `TITLES` (title from `TITLES` or the drift row), then numbered ready records `<prefix>F01`, `<prefix>F02`, ... until the count equals `rec`. The prefix is the code of the stage's first `X` record (in position order) with its trailing digits removed (`R-031` gives `R-`, `CI-09` gives `CI-`, `CI-Q04` gives `CI-Q`, `CI-B02` gives `CI-B`); a stage without `X` records uses its first code in `CHAINS`, else its stage key in upper case followed by `-`; filler titles are "Ready record <code>". Before inserting, the seed collects every code it will write per table and organisation schema and stops with an error naming the duplicates, because codes such as `context_issue_code_uq` are unique per organisation across systems. The stage of a code is taken from `CHAINS` node pairs, else from its prefix as used by the stage's `X` records; an unknown code stops the seed with an error.
